Boulder Civics / City Council / December 18, 2018

December 18, 2018 — City Council Regular Meeting

Regular Meeting December 18, 2018 ai summary
AI Summary

Date: December 18, 2018 Type: Regular Meeting

Meeting Overview

Regular meeting covering Open Space and Mountain Parks accomplishments, waste management education, and extensive public comment on diverse topics including utility oversight, library planning, homelessness, and housing policy.

Key Items

Open Space and Mountain Parks Accomplishments

  • Completed 1.9-mile new trail providing visitor access to Joan or Ranch, north of Boulder open space
  • Rebuilt Neate Valley Trail and constructed new bridge over South Boulder Creek (post-2013 flood recovery)
  • Completed 100+ flood recovery projects
  • Community volunteers: 20,000+ hours of service
  • 630+ guided hikes and educational presentations reaching 15,000 people
  • Treated 146 acres of forest growth; conducted large prescribed fires
  • Facilitated raptor nesting: peregrine falcons, golden eagles, bald eagles
  • Boulder Raptor Monitoring Program: 30th anniversary in 2015
  • 2018: Converted 12 acres for community food production

Waste Management and Zero Waste Initiative

  • 90% of waste could be recycled or composted
  • Contamination issues: plastic bags removed twice daily from recycling equipment; coffee cups second biggest contamination problem
  • Key principle: shapes matter more than recycling numbers for plastics

Utility / Xcel Issues

  • Residential transmission rates increased 9% in last quarter (driven partly by Rush Creek Wind Farm)
  • Xcel Service Company of Colorado 2018 Q3 after-tax net income in Colorado: $463M (~2x 2006 annual profit); growing ~6% annually
  • RFP for renewable energy alternatives: lowest-cost renewable portfolio identified at 89% renewable energy
  • Staff conducting indicative pricing requests on wind and solar at Boulder's scale
  • City continuing legal expenses through PUC system

North Boulder Library Project

  • Project evaluating property between 13th and 14th Streets, Long Left and Canyon Creek
  • Public concerns: lack of road access; suggestion to develop 14th Street from Rosewood Avenue to Violet Avenue
  • Safety concerns: nearby shelters on Lee Hill Road and North Broadway with vandalism, loitering, break-ins, littering; request for security plan and access control policies

Homelessness

  • UN Universal Declaration of Human Rights Article 25 cited (right to adequate housing and food)
  • Benjamin Harvey death referenced
  • Highest poverty in Boulder concentrated in 18–24 year-old demographic (50% of that age group)
  • Property confiscation concerns for homeless individuals

State Legislative Priorities

  • Council attended state legislative breakfast
  • Priorities: minimum wage increase; RTD governance reform; light rail connectivity to regional universities
  • 50,000+ daily commuters in single-occupancy vehicles; insufficient mass transit

Housing and Land Use

  • 60,000 daily commuters in single-occupancy vehicles
  • Community becoming less economically, culturally, and ethnically diverse due to 30+ years of housing scarcity policies
  • Short-term rental regulation discussion: maximum 2 listings per property
  • Horizon West development cited as model for above-55-foot housing in appropriate locations

Outcomes and Follow-Up

  1. City Manager and Police Department to contact resident regarding reported criminal activity and wage theft
  2. Building Department to follow up on permit violations
  3. Public urged to review legislative agenda on Boulder Colorado government website
  4. Council to continue dialogue on North Boulder Library planning
  5. Staff to provide updates on electric utility alternatives and municipal utility strategy
  6. Information packet distributed: electric utility update, Human Services Strategy implementation, land use code change project
